### Page 2

Earnings revision

We factor in 2Q26 and Aug results and revise up our 2026E-28E net income by 15% / 
10% / 8% mainly on higher gross margins, given better product mix (e-labels mainly 
delivered via e-paper while consumer electronics mainly via module, carry a lower GM). 
Overall, we expect 2026E revenues growth at +13% YoY (vs. +12% YoY in 2025A), driven 
by e-labels growing application and adoption, which we model to grow +47% YoY (vs. 
+22% YoY in 2025A), while we also expect a decline in consumer electronics under high 
memory costs (-13% YoY vs. +6% YoY in 2025). We continue to expect revenue growth in 
2027E-28E driven by (1) rising color e-notes / e-readers enlarging size, (2) more stores 
adopting e-labels to save operation costs with growing outdoor signage. On GM, we 
increase our forecasts in 2026E-28E to reflect 2Q26 beat on better product mix.

### Page 3

Valuation

We continue to derive our 12m TP based on a near-term P/E. We derive our target P/E 
via E Ink’s avg. 12m forward PEG ratio since 2017. With largely the same PEG ratio of 
0.5x, 22% forward year earnings growth (vs. 26% previously), our new target P/E is at 
11x (vs. 13x our TP implied 2027E P/E previously). The new target P/E of 11x is largely in 
line with the company’s avg.-1stv. P/E, reflecting the consumer electronics sector 
de-rating under high memory costs. Our new 12m target price is NT$142 (vs. NT$153 
previously). Maintain Neutral.

### Page 4

Price target methodology and risks - E Ink 
Valuation methodology: We are Neutral rated on E Ink. Our 12m target price for E Ink is 
based on a near-term P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. Our 12-m 
TP of NT$142 is based on a target P/E multiple of 11x forward year EPS (2027E). Our 
target P/E multiple of 11x is derived from the company’s avg. PEG since 2017 (0.5x), as 
we expect macro uncertainties to reduce the visibility of e-paper adoption, while we 
remain positive in the long term: technology upgrades (colour, larger sizes) to unlock the 
e-paper market potential. 
Upside / Downside risks: (1) Faster- / slower-than-expected e-readers / e-notes 
penetration rate, (2) Faster- / slower-than-expected ESL adoption rate, (3) Fiercer- / 
healthier-than-expected competition, which could be driven by competition from other 
technology gadgets (e.g. AI / AR glasses, AI PCs, tablets, smart watches, etc.) or new 
entrants in e-paper industry.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

### Page 1

ASE Technology Holding

Continued Capex spree underpins strong 
business outlook

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 800.00 TWD | Price: 703.00 TWD

YTD equipment buys tracks 82% of its '26 capex guidance 
ASE has filed 152 equipment buys and 101 facility construction announcements through 
late September. The spending on equipment YTD has reached NT$273bn, tracking 82% 
of its '26 capex guidance of US$10.5bn (has revised up from US$8.5bn) not yet 
considering their land and facility purchase. The equipment buy should mainly be for 
2.5D packaging and chip probing, reflecting accelerated capacity expansion amid strong 
demand for its LEAP business from its own project wins and its foundry partner’s 
outsourcing. To secure sufficient facility infrastructure to house additional capacity, ASE 
has announced NT$112bn of spending YTD on facility and clean room construction 
contracts, with these projects expected to progressively complete through 2027-2028.  
Solid sales/margins across the board sponsor heavy capex 
Following ASE’s July announcements to acquire new lands and facilities in Kaohsiung and 
Taoyuan, the company announced another NT$2.15bn facility acquisition in Singapore to 
support the future testing expansion needs in its Singapore subsidiary as its major 
customers request supply base diversification. Despite rising depreciation from heavy 
capex investments and additional funding needs, we forecast ASE’s IC ATM sales to 
+37% CAGR from ‘26-28 while GMs should improve to 33.8%/37% in ‘27/28 (from 
28.3% in 2026) on solid packaging and testing demand for AI chipsets (BofAe LEAP 
sales US$4/10/13bn in 2026/27/28) and fast improving mainstream business outlook.  
Decent earnings support re-rating despite funding needs 
We keep Buy on ASE with NT$800 PO on 20x 2H27-1H28 P/E and maintain EPS for 
'26/27/28E at NT$18/31/45. While additional funding may be required to finance its 
aggressive spending on expansion and R&D projects, we believe the decent customer 
commitment and ROE of 25-30%+ from LEAP business should justify the funding costs.

Stock Data  
Price (Common / ADR) 703.00 TWD / 45.31 USD 
Price Objective 800.00 TWD / 51.00 USD

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Established in 1984 and headquartered in Taiwan, ASE 
Technology Holding is the no.1 outsourced assembly and 
testing (OSAT) firm globally, in terms of market share by 
revenue. ASE acquired Siliconware Precision (SPIL) in 2018, 
and had acquired USI in 2010, which became its EMS 
business segment providing synergies in system-level 
packaging. 2 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
We rate ASE Buy, factoring in the company's opportunity in 
advanced packaging and testing evidenced in TSMC's 
outsourcing and its own heavy capex investment. The 
strategy shift should allow the company to capture fast 
growing addressable market in high performance 
computing applications and improves its margins. ASE 
could maintain its leading position in the Outsourced 
Semiconductor Assembly and Test (OSAT) universe, owing 
to its well-established footprints and widespread service 
offerings.

### Page 6

Price objective basis & risk 
ASE Technology Holding (XSRIF / ASX) 
We value ASE Technology Holding at NT$800 per share (US$51 for ADR), based on 20x 
2H27-1H28E P/E, above its historical range of 5-26x, in view of its solidified industry 
position in AI era. We use P/E ratio to value ASE as the company has been able to 
maintain healthy profitability through the cycles in the past 20 years due to 
consolidating industry landscape supporting a more benign supply/demand and keeping 
pricing and margins less cyclical.

Downside risks to our PO are 1) share loss and/or ASP erosion owing to Chinese players' 
cannibalization and/or ASE's worsening execution, 2) weakening end demand due to 
uncontrollable matters, such as macro halt and/or geopolitical tensions, 3) regulatory 
issues that could hinder ASE from generating synergies and benefit its competitors.

iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls.

Document 163: AI角色扮演独角兽Character.ai宣布破产，迪士尼接盘

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI角色扮演独角兽Character.ai宣布破产，迪士尼接盘.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
Character.ai 宣布申请第 11 章破产保护，迪⼠尼作为初始竞标者将以 2000 万美元获得其部
分知识产权许可。破产主要原因为⾯临 “ 重⼤的法律、监管和运营阻⼒ ” ，包括⾼昂的外部模
型使⽤成本及与聊天机器⼈相关的诉讼。
摘要：
曾被视为 AI 领域明星初创公司的 Character.ai 因不堪⾼昂成本与法律诉讼重负，正式申请破产保
护，迪⼠尼将作为初始竞标者介⼊。
据 The Information10 ⽉ 13 ⽇报道，成⽴五年的 AI ⻆⾊扮演初创公司 Character.ai 周⼀宣布，已申
请第 11 章破产保护。
该公司在声明中明确指出，破产是因为遭遇了 “ 重⼤的法律、监管和运营阻⼒ ” 。
作为破产拍卖程序的⼀部分，迪⼠尼将担任初始竞标者。迪⼠尼将⽀付 2000 万美元，以获得

Character.ai 部分知识产权的许可。
同时， Character.ai 表⽰，平台将继续为其订阅⽤⼾提供⽀持服务。
成本与诉讼双重施压
Character.ai 的财务困境主要源于⾼昂的运营成本与沉重的法律诉讼。
在停⽌开发⾃有⼤模型后，该公司不得不依赖外部模型，这带来了巨⼤的使⽤成本。
此外，与聊天机器⼈相关的诉讼也加剧了其财务压⼒。今年 1 ⽉， Character.ai 与部分家庭达成
诉讼和解。这些家庭此前指控该公司的聊天机器⼈导致了其⼦⼥⾃杀或受到其他伤害。
去年，该公司曾考虑过出售或进⾏新⼀轮融资，但最终未能采取任何实质性⾏动。
“ 反向收购 ” 模式的最惨烈结局
Character.ai 的破产，标志着此前在 AI ⾏业盛⾏的 “ 反向收购 ” （ reverse acqui-hire ）模式出现了最
严重的结局。
在这种模式下，初创公司的创始⼈及核⼼团队通常会跳槽⾄⼤型科技公司，⽽⼤公司则通过⽀
付许可费来使⽤初创公司的技术，以此补偿投资者和员⼯股东。⽽ Character.ai 成为 AI ⾏业 “ 反向

收购 ” 浪潮中⾸个以破产收场的案例。
2024 年，⾕歌曾⽀付 27 亿美元以获得 Character.ai 的模型许可，并将联合创始⼈ Noam Shazeer
和 Daniel De Freitas 重新招致麾下。随后， Character.ai 的剩余员⼯接管了公司的所有权。
相⽐之下，其他经历类似模式的 AI 初创公司⼤多选择了业务转型。例如，创始⼈加⼊微软的
Inflection AI ，以及创始⼈加⼊亚⻢逊的 Adept ，均已转向开发其他产品。

Document 180: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trưởng chậm 
nhưng biên lợi nhuận gộp vẫn được 
duy trì do tăng giá bán

Sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 chững lại 
do tâm lý nhập hàng thận trọng và 
hoạt động xây dựng phục hồi chậm

Biên lợi nhuận gộp được dự báo sẽ 
thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Tăng trưởng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất Hoạt động kinh doanh

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

Biểu đồ 3. Tương quan giữa tỷ lệ chi phí bán hàng/doanh 
thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ  
 Biểu đồ 4. Dự phóng nguồn cung bất động sản tại Hà Nội và Hồ 
Chí Minh

Nguồn: BMP, KBSV  Nguồn: CBRE, KBSV

Biểu đồ 5. Vòng quay hàng tồn kho của BMP so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành 
   BMP duy trì vòng quay dẫn đầu trong 
ngành nhờ lợi thế nguồn nguyên liệu và thị 
phần đứng đầu tại miền Nam. Tuy nhiên, 
xu hướng suy giảm các quý gần đây về 
mức 5.3 lần, giảm 0.21 điểm QoQ do nhu 
cầu tiêu thụ toàn ngành suy giảm.

Nguồn: Fiinpro, KBSV

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

gộp của BMP

Document 237: Energy Bills_ Short-term relief, longer-term challenges Utility bill inflation has moderated, but household payments remain elevated and longer-term pressures are building. 2 min r

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy Bills_ Short-term relief, longer-term challenges Utility bill inflation has moderated, but household payments remain elevated and longer-term pressures are building. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13018305     1

Economy 
Energy bills: Short-term relief, longer-term challenges 
16 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Energy bill inflation was around 4% year-over-year (YoY) in August 2026. However, Bank of America internal deposit data shows 
average utility bill payments rose faster, increasing 5% YoY from June through August. This was likely due, in part, to higher 
cooling demand during a warm summer. 
• But bill pressures remain highly uneven across the country. Average utility payments have risen by more than 10% YoY in cities 
such as Detroit, Baltimore and Washington DC, while consumers in Orlando, San Jose and Tampa have seen declines. Some of 
these regional differences appear to reflect policy and infrastructure factors as much as energy usage itself. 
• The long-term outlook still points to upward pressure on electricity bills as demand from data center construction, electrification 
and broader economic activity drive the need for additional generation and grid investment. However, a forecasted strong El 
Niño event this winter could provide some near-term relief, if warmer-than-normal temperatures reduce household heating 
demand, partially offsetting structural cost pressures.

Energy bill inflation has moderated  
Energy is an essential household expense. According to Bureau of Labor Statistics data, energy bills accounted for roughly 3% of 
total household spending as of 2024. US utility prices have been far less affected by higher global oil prices stemming from the 
conflict in the Middle East than gasoline prices, largely because the US is a net exporter of natural gas. Nonetheless, the cost of 
energy has been rising for a prolonged period: since January 2024 the price of electricity has risen 10% and the price of piped 
gas is up 18%.  
The better news is that utility price inflation has moderated relative to the second half of 2025 and early 2026. As of August, 
electricity and piped gas prices were up around 4% year-over-year (YoY). But Bank of America internal deposit data shows that 
average utility payments for electricity and gas were running ahead of inflation in these categories, up 5.3% YoY. One reason 
could be that household demand for cooling was strong this summer, due to relatively warm weather (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: Utility bill payments rose around 5.3% YoY in August  
Consumer prices for electricity and piped gas (% YoY) and three-month average monthly utility payments per customer based on Bank of America internal 
deposit data (% YoY)

Source: Haver Analytics, Bank of America internal data

Energy costs vary widely by region 
While energy bills account for a relatively modest share of household spending nationally, experiences vary across the country. 
Looking at large cities (by Core Based Statistical Areas), our data shows that utility bills rose by more than 10% YoY in Detroit, 
Baltimore and Washington DC from June through August 2026. At the other end of the spectrum, bills fell YoY in Orlando, San 
Joe and Tampa (Exhibit 2).  
Customers’ bills are being pressured by reshoring, reindustrialization, the general pass-through costs in the economy, and a 
significant need for infrastructure replacement in many markets. Regulatory changes and one-off charges have also influenced 
costs in several areas. In Florida, for example, the removal of hurricane recovery surcharges reduced utility bills in 2026, while in 
the Northeast, investments in grid infrastructure and capacity have contributed to higher costs.  
There also remains some disparities in where average bills sit relative to the overall US (Exhibit 3). In Washington DC, Boston, 
Phoenix and Dallas, the average utility bill payment in June through August 2026 was well above the US average. Conversely, 
Chicago, Nashville and Las Vegas paid some of the lowest energy bills relative to the US.

Rising demand for electricity will continue to pressure bills  
Looking longer-term, rising energy demand (particularly for electricity), driven by factors such as data center expansion and 
residential electrification, is likely to keep upward pressure on consumers’ energy bills for a number of years (read Is the AI 
revolution raising consumers' utility bills? and Can the AI buildout keep building? for more).  
Exhibit 4 shows forecasts from the US Energy Information Administration’s August 2026 Short-term Energy Outlook, estimating 
a 4% YoY rise in commercial and industrial electricity consumption. Accommodating this growth will likely require ongoing 
investment in grid capacity and power generation, and some of these costs may be passed on to consumers through higher 
energy bills.  
Exhibit 4: The demand for electricity is rising particularly from commercial and industrial sectors 
US electrical demand (in billion kilowatt hours) 2020-2027E (estimates/forecast from 2026)

Source: US Energy Information Administration 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Short-term, the weather could prove important  
El Niño is the warming phase of ENSO (El Niño–Southern Oscillation), a global climate phenomenon that emerges from variation 
in winds and sea surface temperatures over the Pacific Ocean. El Niño events can have widespread and profound global impacts, 
causing extreme weather such as storms, floods and higher temperatures. The strongest recorded El Niño took place in 1877 to 
1878 and led to a global famine that killed more than 50 million people.  
The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) believes there is a high probability that a very strong El Niño 
event will occur this year (Exhibit 5). One way to put this in historical context is through the NOAA’s Relative Oceanic Niño Index 
(RONI). A positive RONI indicates the central/eastern tropical Pacific is unusually warm relative to the rest of the tropics. A 
higher RONI indicates a more severe El Niño, though NOAA points out that uncertainty on impacts also tends to rise with RONI.  
Exhibit 5: The NOAA attaches a very high probability to a severe El Niño event over coming months  
Likelihood of an El Nino event estimated by severity (%)

Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA)

Exhibit 6 shows that the current forecast for RONI from October to December 2026 is one of the largest on record. In recent 
history, similar-sized El Niño events have only occurred in 1997-98, 2015-16 and to a lesser extent in 2022-23.  
Exhibit 6: The El Niño event in 2026 is forecasted to be the largest on record 
Relative Oceanic Niño Index (RONI) for October-December by year (2026: NOAA 50% probability forecast for October-December 2026)

Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) 
Note: Red bars: 1997-1998/2015-16. Yellow bar: 2026-27 50% forecast. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
For the US, significant El Niño events tend to bring higher-than-usual temperatures and more rainfall (Exhibit 7). However, no El 
Niño is exactly the same. For example, in 1997-98 California was flooded in but in 2015-16 it was dry, while the nation overall 
was wet. Both time periods were among the highest levels of RONI in the past 70 years.  
Exhibit 7: El Niño events have been noted for higher temperatures and rainfall

I NSTI TUTE

What does this mean for consumers and their energy bills? The most predictable impact from a severe El Niño appears to be 
unseasonably warm temperatures, implying reduced heating needs during the fall and winter. Consumers could therefore receive 
some relief in their utility bills and potentially have additional income to spend on discretionary goods and services over the 
coming months.  
There is a large caveat, however. If El Niño manifests in stormy and rainy conditions – likely impacting the southern parts of the 
US – then some households may end up having to spend on repairs and maintenance following weather damage (read more on 
consumer spending in the aftermath of natural disasters in October’s What's the impact of natural disasters on local 
economies?). This might boost consumer discretionary spending, but not in a way that implies improved well-being. And longer 
term, the broader impact of both climate change and energy demands from the AI revolution are likely to keep upward pressure 
on bills.

### Page 6

  I NSTI TUTE

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 252: France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01)

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

2027 Budget: Fiscal consolidation must now resume

The 2027 budget, adopted by the French Council of Ministers today, calls for a reduction in France’s fiscal 
deficit to 5% of GDP in 2027 (down from 5.4% in 2026). This is a first step toward s the multi-year fiscal 
consolidation needed to stabilise the public debt-to-GDP ratio. This ratio is expected to rise further in 2027 
and until the deficit is reduced to 3% of GDP. Furthermore, given the increase in some expenditures (+0.6 pp 
of GDP for debt service and defence spending), the projected fiscal effort amounts to 1 pp. Nearly one-third 
of this effort would come from higher compulsory levies, and two -thirds from curbing government and 
social spending.

2027 Draft finance law: A step in the right direction. The government is forecasting an improvement in the fiscal 
deficit from 5.4% of GDP in 2026 to 5% in 2027. This projection is based on a credible growth assumption of 1% 
(see our analysis of French growth drivers). To achieve this target, compulsory levies (CL) are expected to rise to 
44.2% of GDP, an increase of 0.3 pp (the same increase as in 2026, compared with +0.9 pp in 2025). The public 
spending-to-GDP ratio would decline by 0.2 pp to 56.9%. The effort required to reduce the deficit to 5% is greater 
than it appears. Indeed, some expenditures will increase (interest payments by 0.4 pp and defence spending by 
0.2 pp). Consequently, the fiscal effort for next year amounts not to 0.4 pp, but to 1 pp of GDP. One-third of this will 
be achieved through increased CL, and two-thirds through spending cuts. Social spending will contribute, notably 
through the cancellation of EUR 3 billion in social contribution reductions for low-wage earners, as well as efforts 
totaling EUR 6 billion each in the areas of pensions and healthcare. Social spending shows the largest deviation 
from pre-COVID levels, with an increase representing 1.2 pp of GDP, nearly half of the gap between the 2026 
budget balance and a 3% deficit. Government spending, excluding interest payments and defence, is expected to 
be frozen in nominal terms.

A compromise seems likely despite a tight schedule. The French National Assembly will start reviewing the 
budget on Tuesday 13 October. The government will need to ensure that a majority vote against the draft budget 
does not form. The current circumstances, including increasing sovereign bond yields and the upcoming 
presidential election, call for a consensus based on fiscal consolidation of the size proposed by the government. If 
Parliament does not pass a budget by the end of the year, the government will have to choose between 
implementing the government’s budget proposal by executive order ( only possible if there was no parliamentary 
vote) or through a special law (as in 2025 and 2026). A special law would enable tax collection , while the 
government and social security agencies could continue to issue debt. In this case, the 2026 budget would apply 
in 2027 (without indexation) until Parliament passes a budget in the first few weeks of the year. This must occur 
before the end of February (when Parliament’s session ends, earlier than usual due to the presidential election).

The one-year postponement of the 5% budget deficit target has a measurable impact. The gap between the 
budget adopted for 2026 (deficit of 5% of GDP) and the actual budget outcome (deficit of 5.4%) is due to 
unforeseeable circumstances (the conflict in Iran  and extreme drought). However, postponing the deficit target  
previously set for 2026 to 2027 comes at a cost: 1) the public debt-to-GDP ratio is expected to reach 121% in 2027 
according to our forecasts (1 pp above our scenario with a 5% target in 2026 and a deficit reduced to 4.5% in 2027), 
and 2) the (upcoming) postponement to 2032 of the 3% of GDP deficit target (a commitment that most presidential 
candidates plan to make) rather than in 2029 (the government had committed to this with the European Commission 
in 2024). In 2032, the public debt-to-GDP ratio would stabilise at 124% of GDP (instead of 121.5% if the deficit had 
been reduced to 5% of GDP as early as 2026 and to 3% of GDP by the end of the decade).

Another delay in fiscal consolidation would further erode France’s credibility. This new delay could stem 
either from a decision by the government formed in May 2027 to immediately implement part of its agenda, or from 
the enactment of a special law that would remain in effect until (just after) the likely legislative elections in June  
2027  or  for  the  entire  year.  If  the  government  emerging  from  the  elections  were  to  resume  fiscal  
consolidation only in 2028 (with a deficit of 5% of GDP that it projects to reduce to 3% by 2032), public debt would 
reach 126% of GDP by that date (a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in an additional 2 pp of 
debt). This further delay could potentially undermine the credibility of the next government, given that budget 
targets were  not met in three out of four years between 2023 and 2026, a situation that would be costly amid a 
sharp rise in long-term interest rates. Furthermore, a higher deficit would increase the amount of debt to be issued 
within an already  tight schedule. The Agence France Trésor (AFT) has announced debt issuances totaling EUR 
340 billion in 2027 (an  increase  of  EUR  20  billion  compared  to  2026).  This  increase  is  due  to  the  maturing  
COVID  related  debt (beginning in 2027 and continuing largely through 2030), while the amount of debt to be 
issued to finance the deficit will remain stable compared to 2026. A decrease from this last driver would be needed 
in subsequent years so that  the AFT’s debt issuance can stabilise.

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of the Advanced Economies Team, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Executive summary

台灣半導體暨科技產業公布 9 ⽉份及 3Q26 營收，我們以公布的數據看產業趨勢
變化及公司與彭博社分析師預期進⾏分析⽐較，投資⼈須審慎評估。總⽽⾔
之， 3Q26 營收明顯超過彭博社分析師預期的公司⼤幅多於低於預期的公司達 6
倍多，⽽超過 53% 年增（⽤晶圓代⼯的 9 ⽉份營收年增做標竿）的有 6 個次產
業。除此以外，我們還提供了 3Q26 營收年增明顯改善及惡化的前 20 名公司整
理及排序，及 3Q26 營收明顯季增 / 季減的前 20 名公司整理及排序。總結⽽
⾔，伺服器產業鏈（對標公司有 Nvidia, AMD, Intel, ⾕歌 TPU ， Dell,
SuperMicro) ，存儲記憶體（對標公司有三星 , SK Hynix, SanDisk,
Western Digital) ， CCL/PCB ，半導體建廠及測試產業 3Q26 營收（對標公司
有  Teradyne, Advantest, Form Factor ）明顯⾼於分析師預期， 3Q26 營收
年增改善明顯的有存儲記憶體，光纖通訊（對標公司有 Lumentum,

Fabrinet, Coherent ）， CoWoS/CoPoS 先進封測設備產業。 3Q26 營收季增
明顯改善的產業有 CoWoS/CoPoS 先進封測設備產業，設計服務（對標公司有
Broadcom, Marvell) ，存儲記憶體，光纖通訊，及伺服器產業鏈。

Document 265: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova
Date:    8 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL:
French Toast & Brazilian Bossa
Nova
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 10 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Insaanu Studio / Unsplash

I. Bonds
The global bond market remains jittery, though yields may be starting to
stabilize after their rout in recent months (chart). Nevertheless, geopolitical
turbulence may still buffet them.

### Page 2

Yields have risen for an assortment of reasons, from better-than-expected
economic growth (US) to fiscal instability concerns (France). Brazil and
China are the only major countries showing declines in bond yields so far
this year (chart).
Bond yields have also risen on higher-for-longer oil prices as the war in
the Middle East has continued to disrupt oil supplies. The Houthis have
said airliners should avoid Saudi airspace, declaring it a zone of military

operations after the Saudi-backed Yemeni government launched an
offensive  against the group earlier this month. On Saturday, a third attack
this week on Riyadh's international airport left 12 people dead and dozens
wounded. President Donald Trump said the US will "look at" joining Saudi
strikes on Houthis.

### Page 3

II. Stay Home vs Go Global
So far this year, the US has held onto its 65% market-cap share of the All
Country World (ACW) MSCI (chart). Japan's share has also held steady at
5%. However, Europe's share has slipped (13.3%) to the advantage of the
share of the Emerging Markets (12.0%).
Nevertheless, last week, the US outperformed the Developed World ex-
US as the French debt crisis weighed on European stock prices (chart).
So far this month, the US MSCI is the second-best-performing stock price

index we track (chart). Brazil was the best-performing in response to first-
round presidential election results showing conservative Flavio Bolsonaro,
son of former President  Jair Bolsonaro, exceeding expectations in the
race against leftist President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva.

### Page 4

III. Earnings & Valuation
The ratio of forward earnings for the US MSCI to the ACW ex US MSCI
fell through the summer this year (chart). It stopped doing so in recent
weeks.
Forward P/Es have been falling worldwide since the start of the year, as
forward earnings have outpaced stock price gains (chart).

### Page 5

IV. Currencies
The French debt crisis is also weighing on the euro, which is down from
$1.20 at the start of this year to $1.12 (chart).
As a result, the DXY dollar index has rebounded to its best reading since
early 2025 (chart). We remain constructive on the US dollar.

### Page 6

V. Notable
(1) German factory orders fell sharply in August, plunging 10.6% m/m
(chart). The primary driver behind the contraction was a steep pullback in
large-scale contracts. The headline drop was almost entirely due to a
61.5% slump in large-scale orders for aircraft, ships, trains, and military
vehicles, which suffered a major correction following an exceptional surge
in July. When these volatile large-scale contracts are excluded, new
manufacturing orders actually slipped by just 0.1%, indicating that core

industrial demand was essentially treading water rather than experiencing
a broad-based collapse.
(2) CQQQ continues to underperform QQQ (chart). While China's long-
term industrial policy goals are ambitious, cyclical demand inside China

### Page 7

has struggled to generate the same explosive revenue growth seen in
Western tech sectors. Furthermore, China's tech industries carry a
persistent regulatory discount. Chinese technology sectors have
historically been vulnerable to abrupt policy shifts, anti-monopoly
crackdowns, data security reviews, and ongoing geopolitical tensions
(including US–China trade restrictions and semiconductor export
controls), which compress valuations and deter long-term institutional

clause.

### Page 8

Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

Document 285: Lots Of FEMO In Q3's Earnings Reporting Season

Source PDF: all report pdf/Lots Of FEMO In Q3's Earnings Reporting Season.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Lots Of FEMO In Q3's Earnings Reporting Season
Date:    4 hours ago
Lots Of FEMO In Q3's Earnings Reporting Season

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Lots Of FEMO In Q3's Earnings
Reporting Season
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 8 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Taya Dianna / Unsplash

I. FEMO in Q3
We have spent much of the past few months marveling at Corporate
America's Fabulous Earnings Momentum (FEMO). S&P 500 earnings per
share growth approached 30% y/y in Q1 and accelerated to more than
50% in Q2, although unusually large mark-to-market investment gains
boosted both figures. Even excluding those gains, earnings grew roughly

### Page 2

20% and 25%, respectively. Q2 also marked the fifth consecutive quarter
with record earnings.
Q3 is shaping up to be another blockbuster quarter. As Joe reported this
week, industry analysts' consensus forecast for Q3-2026 S&P 500 EPS
growth started the quarter at an already remarkable 27.6% y/y. Rather
than declining as it typically does during the quarter, it climbed 3.0 ppts to
30.6%. The level of expected Q3 EPS rose 2.2% over the quarter, ranking

as the 12th-largest upward revision in the 130 quarters since Q2-1994.
That's no small feat!
Better yet, FEMO is broadening across Corporate America. Analysts
expect all 11 S&P 500 sectors to deliver positive y/y growth in both
revenues and earnings. Only once before, in Q2-2021, has such a perfect
sweep occurred in the 25 years we've tracked the data. Overall, S&P 500
revenues are expected to grow 11.6% y/y, compared with earnings growth
of 30.6% (chart).

Energy is expected to lead Q3 earnings growth at a whopping 114.7% y/y,
followed by Information Technology, Communication Services, and
Materials (chart). Energy's expected earnings growth has surged from
negative territory earlier this year, while Information Technology's has
climbed steadily, reflecting the AI investment boom.

### Page 3

II. Financials in Q3
Q3 earnings season kicks off next week with the big banks. The latest
banking data suggest there is plenty to be optimistic about.
Commercial and industrial loans rose 9.7% y/y during the week of
September 23, while total bank loans and leases increased a robust 7.6%
(chart). Both point to strong bank lending.
Large domestic banks are leading the charge, with loan growth of 7.6%
y/y compared with 5.5% at smaller banks (chart). Strong bank lending is

another reflection of a resilient economy.

### Page 4

Commercial banks' allowances for loan and lease losses stood at $203
billion during the week of September 23, little changed over the past two
years, confirming that banks do not see a deterioration in credit quality.
Meanwhile, new US corporate bond and equity issuance reached a record
$3.1 trillion over the 12 months through August (chart). That's good news
for investment banking activity and another sign of robust demand for
capital across Corporate America.

### Page 5

III. What could go wrong?
All this sounds bullish, and it is! But our worry list remains long, not least
because of the ongoing conflict in the Middle East. The bid-ask spread
between Washington and Tehran remains wide. The risk of a military re-
escalation is increasing. For now, we expect energy prices to remain
higher for longer, keeping inflation and bond yields elevated (chart).
Meanwhile, global bond yields keep rising (chart). In the US, stronger

nominal GDP growth and a higher neutral rate partly explain the rise. The
energy shock, the unwinding yen-carry trade, and mounting concerns
about fiscal excesses are adding fuel to the fire. In France, the Bond
Vigilantes are pushing yields above nominal GDP growth amid high debt,
weak growth, and political gridlock. The risk is contagion across Europe.

### Page 6

Other worries include second-round inflation effects, more aggressive Fed
rate hikes, rising US debt-service costs, an AI investment slowdown, post-
midterm political gridlock, and mega IPOs draining liquidity from the stock
market.
Against this backdrop, we recently moved our 8,400 target for the S&P
500 from the end of this year to the middle of next year. Importantly, we
haven't lowered our earnings outlook. S&P 500 forward EPS reached a
record $406.45 on October 1, and we still expect it to climb to $425 by

year-end (chart). Instead, we reduced our target forward P/E from 19.8 to
18.6 to reflect valuation risks.
💡

### Page 7

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

Source PDF: all report pdf/Marvell推出第二代CXL内存扩展器，最高支持9TB DDR4内存.pdf

Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

Marvell 发布第⼆代 CXL 内存扩展器，单控制器最⾼⽀持 9TB DDR4 或 6TB DDR5 内存，压
缩吞吐量提升⾄ 250GB/s ，旨在缓解⼤模型推理的内存瓶颈，并提升 AI 基础设施效率。
AMD 、 Arm 、阿⾥巴巴、三星及 SK 海⼒⼠等参与⽣态合作，新产品预计于 2027 年⼀季度
提供样品。
摘要：
Marvell 正通过更⼤容量、更⾼带宽的内存扩展⽅案，瞄准 AI 推理基础设施升级带来的需求增
⻓。
据彭博报道， 10 ⽉ 12 ⽇，Marvell 正式发布第⼆代 Structera X 3406 和 3506 CXL 内存扩展器。
公司称，这是业界⾸款六通道 CXL 3.2 内存扩展器，单控制器最⾼⽀持 9TB DDR4 或 6TB DDR5
内存，内存通道数量较上⼀代增加 50% 。两款产品预计于 2027 年第⼀季度开始向客⼾提供样
品。
此次发布旨在应对 AI 推理⼯作负载⽇益突出的内存瓶颈。随着⼤型语⾔模型（ LLM ）规模扩⼤、
上下⽂窗⼝延⻓以及 KV 缓存需求快速增⻓，传统服务器架构的内存容量与带宽正⾯临更⼤压

⼒，内存逐渐成为制约 AI 性能和基础设施效率的关键因素。
AMD 、 Arm 、阿⾥巴巴、三星和 SK 海⼒⼠等企业均对新产品及相关⽣态合作表⽰⽀持。
核⼼技术升级：更多通道，更⼤容量
Structera X 3406 和 3506 在上⼀代产品基础上实现了内存通道、扩展容量及压缩性能等⽅⾯的升
级。
在内存配置⽅⾯，两款产品均⽀持六通道内存。其中， 3406 ⽀持 18 个 DDR4 RDIMM 插槽，最⾼
容量达 9TB ； 3506 ⽀持 12 个 DDR5 RDIMM 插槽，最⾼容量为 6TB 。在主机互联⽅⾯，两款产品
均⽀持最多四台主机共享内存资源，主机互联带宽最⾼可达 256GB/s ，采⽤ PCIe 6.0 x16 接⼝。
在压缩性能⽅⾯，新产品内置 LZ4 压缩引擎，压缩和解压缩吞吐量最⾼达 250GB/s ，较上⼀代提
升 5 倍，可将有效内存容量扩⼤⾄原来的 2 ⾄ 3 倍。
Marvell 表⽰，在⼤型语⾔模型推理场景中，新产品可在相同上下⽂⻓度下，将⾸字节响应时间

（ TTFT ）缩短 40% ⾄ 50% ；在深度学习推荐模型（ DLRM ）场景中，单控制器每秒查询量最⾼
可提升⾄原来的 2 倍，嵌⼊表有效容量则可扩⼤⾄原来的 2 ⾄ 3 倍。
瞄准超⼤规模数据中⼼，⽣态合作持续扩展
Marvell 表⽰，第⼆代 Structera X 沿⽤已向超⼤规模数据中⼼客⼾出货的第⼀代 2404/2504 产品
的软件栈和⽣态集成⽅案，旨在加快新产品的客⼾导⼊进程。
多家⾏业伙伴公开表达了⽀持。AMD 企业副总裁 Raghu Nambiar 表⽰，双⽅的合作依托 EPYC
处理器的性能与可扩展性，帮助企业在 AI 和数据密集型应⽤需求增⻓的背景下扩展内存容量、提
⾼资源利⽤效率。
Arm 云端 AI go-to-market 副总裁 Eddie Ramirez 表⽰， Structera X 将 CXL 3.2 、内存扩展和压缩
能⼒引⼊ Arm ⽣态，为超⼤规模数据中⼼⾼效运⾏复杂 AI ⼯作负载提供更多选择。

Document 297: Nuclear energy_ One industry, many reactors Our latest primer explores the reactor technologies and hurdles shaping nuclear’s next chapter. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Nuclear energy_ One industry, many reactors Our latest primer explores the reactor technologies and hurdles shaping nuclear’s next chapter. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13022014     1

Sustainability 
Nuclear energy: One industry, many reactors 
29 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• As power demand grows, nuclear has returned to the forefront of the energy conversation. Yet the industry is far from one-size-
fits-all, encompassing a range of reactor technologies designed to serve different markets, applications and deployment models. 
• Nuclear power is evolving beyond traditional large-scale reactors. While proven designs continue to anchor utility-scale 
generation, small modular reactors (SMRs) and microreactors are being developed to serve a broader range of applications 
through more flexible, modular deployment models, though most remain in the demonstration phase today. 
• The future of nuclear deployment will depend on more than reactor design alone. Supply chains, skilled labor, fuel availability and 
regulatory approvals remain critical challenges, though recent policy initiatives are intended to support licensing, fuel production 
and broader industry growth.

Nuclear energy has re-emerged at the center of the power conversation, driven by rising electricity demand and tightening 
supply-demand dynamics across the grid. Yet nuclear is not a single technology. It encompasses a wide range of reactor designs 
and sizes, each with distinct economics, risks and deployment pathways. Building off our previous nuclear publication (which can 
be found here: Nuclear energy: Who, what, where, when, why?), this primer unpacks that complexity, grounding the broader 
nuclear narrative in the practical realities of development, from technology and construction to fuel, licensing and execution.  
Reactor sizes 
Nuclear power is best understood as a collection of reactor classes differentiated by scale, deployment model and end use 
(Exhibit 1). Rather than competing directly with one another, these technologies are designed to serve different segments of the 
power market and, in many cases, can scale alongside one another. 
Exhibit 1: Different reactor types have different advantages and risks 
Comparison of reactor technologies

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Key Distinctions  
• Large reactors (≥1 gigawatt (GW)): Designed for large-scale electricity generation, these reactors provide consistent, high-
capacity power and serve as core assets within the grid. 
• Small modular reactors (SMRs)(~50–300 megawatts (MW)): Mid-scale systems designed for modular deployment across 
utilities, industrial users and large commercial customers, offering greater flexibility in siting and capacity expansion.  
• Microreactors (≤20 MW): Small, highly distributed systems intended for niche or off-grid applications where reliability, 
autonomy or energy security are primary considerations. 
Technology 
Nuclear power generation is based on a common principle: heat produced from nuclear fission is used to generate steam, which 
drives a turbine to create electricity (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: In a nuclear reactor, heat is formed by fission 
Process for a nuclear fission reaction

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Nuclear reactor designs can be grouped based on coolant type, neutron spectrum and fuel configuration. Most reactors currently 
operating in the US are light-water reactors (LWRs), though a wide range of advanced designs are being developed (Exhibit 3).  
Exhibit 3: Gen III & Gen IV+ technologies have different characteristics 
Reactor technologies 
Reactor Type Coolant Moderator Neutron Spectrum Operating Temperature Pressure 
Pressurized Water Reactor (PWR) Water Light water Thermal ~300–330°C High (~150 bar) 
Boiling Water Reactor (BWR) Water Light water Thermal ~280–290°C High (lower than PWR) 
Heavy Water Reactor (HWR) Heavy water Heavy water Thermal ~300°C High 
Molten Salt Reactor (MSR) Molten salt Graphite (typical) Thermal / fast (design-dependent) ~600–750°C Low 
High-Temperature Gas Reactor (HTGR) Helium gas Graphite Thermal ~700–800°C Low 
Sodium Fast Reactor (SFR) Liquid sodium None Fast ~450–550°C Low 
Gas-Cooled Fast Reactor (GFR) Helium gas None Fast ~700–850°C Low 
Source: BofA Global Research, Energy Information Administration (EIA), Idaho National Laboratory (INL), Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
AP1000 
The AP1000 is a Generation III+ pressurized water reactor with a capacity of approximately 1.1 GW per unit (Exhibit 4). Its design 
is centered around passive safety systems that rely on gravity, natural circulation and compressed gas, rather than large numbers 
of active pumps and diesel-backed safety systems. By reducing the number of safety-critical moving parts, this approach lowers 
system complexity and supports a more standardized large-reactor platform.

### Page 3

29 September 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: AP1000s typically need about 1,000 acres of land 
Diagram of an AP1000 nuclear plant

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
The AP1000’s design emphasizes open-top construction, with large structural and equipment modules manufactured offsite and 
then assembled at the project site. This approach is intended to reduce onsite labor needs, improve quality control through 
factory fabrication and support a more repeatable, standardized construction process. 
The AP1000 is no longer a conceptual technology. It is a licensed, utility-scale nuclear design already operating in the field. As a 
result, the key remaining considerations center on construction costs, project execution and the economics of deploying 
additional units, according to BofA Global Research. 
SMRs 
Small modular reactors (SMRs) are nuclear power plants generally 300 MWe or less per unit and are designed around a factory-
based, modular construction model (Exhibit 5). Unlike traditional gigawatt-scale nuclear projects, SMRs represent a shift toward 
repeatable, standardized deployment (read our prior SMR publication: Q&A quick guide: Small modular reactors). Major 
components can be manufactured in a factory setting and then transported to project sites for assembly, reducing the amount of 
onsite construction required. Their standardized designs also create opportunities for learning-by-doing, potentially lowering 
costs over time. In addition, capacity can be added incrementally through discrete modules, allowing capital to be deployed in 
stages rather than committed upfront.

### Page 4

4 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 5: SMRs typically require 50-200 acres of land, depending on design and location 
Diagram of an SMR

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Market landscape  
There are dozens of SMR designs globally, with more than 100 projects at various stages of development, though only a limited 
number are currently operational or under construction. While developers are pursuing a range of reactor technologies, light-
water reactor (LWR) designs still dominate the US development landscape (Exhibit 6). According to BofA Global Research, the 
sector remains largely in a development and demonstration phase rather than broad commercial deployment. 
Exhibit 6: LWRs make up 50% of the SMR designs 
US SMR designs by technology type (%)

Source: Energy Information Administration (EIA), BofA Global Research

I NSTI TUTE

Microreactors 
Microreactors are nuclear power systems typically 20 MWe or less per unit, representing the smallest class of advanced reactors 
(Exhibit 7). They emphasize flexibility, allowing deployment where traditional grid-scale infrastructure is impractical. Many 
designs incorporate multi-year fuel cycles that reduce refueling requirements and associated logistics burdens, making them 
particularly attractive for remote communities, industrial sites and defense-related applications. 
Exhibit 7: Microreactors need about one to five acres of land depending on the design 
Diagram of a microreactor

Source: BofA Global Research, Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Market landscape  
A growing number of developers are pursuing microreactor technologies in the US, however, despite increasing interest, most 
projects remain in the demonstration and pilot phase rather than commercial deployment. According to BofA Global Research, 
federal government programs continue to play a central role in funding and advancing early deployments, with several initial 
projects targeted for later this decade. 
Supply chain 
Large reactors depend on specialized components and complex construction processes, limiting the number of qualified 
suppliers. Modular designs, on the other hand, shift a greater share of manufacturing from construction sites to factory 
environments, with the goal of improving consistency, reducing onsite complexity and enabling more standardized deployment. 
As reactor sizes decrease, component requirements generally become less specialized, expanding the potential supplier base and 
simplifying project execution. 
Workforce availability and quality assurance remain critical across all reactor types. Large projects require significant numbers of 
skilled tradespeople, including welders, electricians and nuclear-certified inspectors. The US nuclear workforce has declined over 
prior decades, leading to labor shortages during first-of-a-kind builds.  
Fuel 
Fuel is one of the most important building blocks in the advanced nuclear ecosystem, as availability, manufacturing capacity and 
regulatory approval are all critical to deployment. Because many next-generation reactors require specialized fuels, the

### Page 6

6 29 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

development of commercial-scale fuel supply chains will be a key factor in determining how quickly advanced nuclear 
technologies can be brought to market. 
HALEU 
High-assay low-enriched uranium (HALEU) refers to uranium enriched above the roughly 5% U-235 content used in conventional 
reactors but below the 20% threshold for highly-enriched uranium. HALEU enables smaller reactor cores and longer fuel cycles, 
making it a key requirement for many advanced reactor designs. 
Historically, commercial HALEU supply has been limited. To address this gap, the Department of Energy launched the HALEU 
Availability Program in 2024 to support enrichment, fuel processing and deconversion capacity. The government is also working 
to recover HALEU from existing materials, with a targeted recovery of roughly 10 to 26 metric tons over time. 
The first US HALEU production in decades occurred in 2023, though output remains limited. Current production is approximately 
900 kilograms annually and is government-owned, with material allocated primarily to research, fuel qualification and early 
demonstration projects. 
TRISO 
Tri-structural isotropic particle fuel (TRISO) is key for high-temperature gas-cooled reactors (HTGRs) and certain microreactors. 
Unlike conventional nuclear fuel, it requires specialized manufacturing and therefore is currently only limited to pilot-scale 
production. But key development efforts include DOE-led research through the Advanced Gas Reactor (AGR) program and 
expansion of private-sector manufacturing with DOE support. 
Regulatory approval is also required for new fuel designs, manufacturing processes and transportation systems. As a result, 
scaling TRISO production remains an important step toward the broader deployment of advanced reactors. 
Fuel Risk 
Fuel availability is a key execution risk for advanced nuclear projects. Many next-generation reactor designs rely on HALEU or 
TRISO fuel, both of which remain supply-constrained. As a result, reactor technology may be ready before fuel supply is, creating 
a potential bottleneck for deployment.  
Challenges include multi-year lead times for fuel production and certification, potential gaps between reactor licensing and fuel 
readiness, and the limited supplier base and transportation requirements associated with HALEU. Together, these factors can 
add uncertainty to project timelines and costs until fuel supply chains reach commercial scale. 
Licensing 
Licensing is a key determinant of nuclear project timelines, capital deployment and execution risk. Regulatory requirements 
shape not only whether a project is approved, but also how predictable construction schedules are and how easily reactor 
designs can be replicated. In the US, commercial reactors are licensed by the Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC), whose 
framework emphasizes safety reviews, environmental assessments and public participation. While this approach provides 
regulatory certainty, it can also create lengthy approval timelines. 
Large light-water reactors benefit from well-established regulatory processes, while frameworks for advanced reactors continue 
to evolve. For first-of-a-kind projects, licensing can extend four to eight years or longer depending on technology maturity, 
application quality, public intervention and regulatory resources.  
Policy  
Recent years have seen a flurry of initiatives to revitalize nuclear energy. A series of coordinated policy moves, demonstration 
programs, fuel supply efforts and industry partnerships have converged to create the conditions for a potential nuclear 
resurgence:  
• ADVANCE Act: Signed into law in July 2024, the ADVANCE (Accelerating Deployment of Versatile, Advanced Nuclear for 
Clean Energy) Act modernizes the NRC licensing process (making it more affordable and predictable). The legislation also  
facilitates the siting of new reactors at brownfield sites, supports domestic fuel-cycle capabilities, and includes broader NRC 
institutional reforms, such as workforce recruitment and retention initiatives, updates to the agency’s mission statement and 
provisions related to international nuclear cooperation and exports. 
• Executive orders: On May 23, 2025, the Trump administration issued four executive orders aimed at accelerating US nuclear 
deployment through licensing reform, expedited reactor testing, expanded national security applications and efforts to 
strengthen the domestic nuclear fuel and supply chain base. Since their issuance, federal agencies, particularly the 
Department of Energy, have taken steps to implement these directives. While progress has been made on several fronts, a 
number of the orders' longer-term objectives remain under active implementation, according to BofA Global Public Policy.

• 8月中國經濟數據仍顯示
外貿強 ，內需疲軟的景
象。除反應信心不足 、
內需疲軟外 ，反內卷的

• 中國經濟現階段來自價

期，內需疲軟將視財政
執行力道進程才能緩解
接下來的景氣下行壓力 。
《投資關鍵-中國經濟K型化情勢依舊》
縱觀8月中國經濟數據仍顯示外貿強，內需疲軟的景
象。中國8月出口增速從前月23.9%回升至25%，究
其細項，科技相關以名目計算年增速續保高速 ，如
自動資料處理機器零件年增76.5%， 儲存組件年增
159.2%，印刷電路板年增 44.4%與積體電路年增
129.8%。惟若以數量計算年增大多回落，顯示科技

8月出口增速佳來自價格貢獻。另一留意低端消費出
口也見改善，可觀察歐美消費旺季的挹注力道。
8月零售銷售持續疲軟年增回落至 0.4%，汽車零售
年衰退幅度擴大至-18.5%是主要拖累，零組件價格
上揚帶動通訊設備零售增27.3%。
8月實質工業生產年增略好預期至 5.2%，科技相關
保持較高能見度 ，通訊、電腦及其他電子設備年增
17.2%，但如汽車 、粗鋼與水泥較反應內需表現黯
淡，反內卷政策制約太陽能板等生產表現 。固定資
產投資年衰退幅度擴大至 -7.2%，房地產投資雙位
數衰退趨勢不變 ，製造業投資年減 -2.3%，我們認
為除反應信心不足 、內需疲軟外 ，反內卷的制約以
及政策傾斜高端科技是主因。
總體來看，中國經濟現階段來自價格為主的出口貢
獻，景氣越趨依賴全球科技週期 ，內需疲軟將視財
政執行力道進程才能緩解接下來的景氣下行壓力。
出口雖穩健，但內需與投資乏力，產能利用不振

《投資關鍵-台灣連十季利率不動 ，但升息壓力增 》
台灣央行第三季理監事會議宣布重貼現率、擔保放
款融通利率與短期融通利率維持不變在 2.0%、
2.375%和4.25%；可轉讓定期存單 (NCD) 28天期、
91 天期 、 182 天 期 也 維 持1.215% 、 1.275% 和
1.395% 不變。2024年以來，台灣央行持續保持利
率十連凍紀錄。
不動產信用管制部分 ，由於截至7月底，全體銀行
不動產貸款集中度續降至34.44%，低於2020年12

月底(第1度調整信用管制時點 )之 36.64%)，因此
調升自然人第 2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限至 7成。
同時也刪除購地貸款有關切結於一定期間內動工興
建之規定，相關規範回歸銀行依授信實務辦理。
資料來源：台灣央行，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09/18。

重點摘要
6
• 本行認為台灣景氣動能佳
且增長更加均衡 ，形成經
濟的正向循環 ，台灣經濟
環境已有宏觀的升息條件 。
年以來相較各國重返寬鬆貨幣政策列車 ，台灣依
舊維持基準利率 2%，2024/7各類新台幣存款準
備率調升 1碼。另觀察台灣 7月M2年增速 7.42%
(今年以來6.6%)雖高於政策目標區間 2.5%~6.5%，
但低於17.8%的名目 GDP 增長率，意味貨幣需求
增長速度超過貨幣供應量 ，因此台灣貨幣政策環
境已偏緊縮，所以並不需要立即升息。
展望後市，隨AI應用擴散至推論 、代理及終端裝
置推升算力需求 ，AI投資建設的暢旺驅動相關硬
體升級與用量成長 ，台灣企業涵蓋重要 AI供應鏈
節點，強勁出口引領經濟成長。

觀察AI科技紅利也逐步外溢其他產業 ，非科技如
機械出口逐步加溫 。相關半導體投資擴產需求積
極，投資成長也助力台灣經濟 ，內需也因股市財
富效應帶動。台灣景氣動能佳且增長更加均衡 ，
形成經濟的正向循環。
資料來源：主計處，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/08/07。
AI科技紅利外溢，消費與投資對景氣貢獻逐步擴大

從央行聲明觀察對於左右貨幣政策的觀察事項 ，
分別為國內通膨發展 ，主要經濟體貨幣政策緊縮
程度，國內金融情勢 、其他不確定性如 AI應用前
景。相較先前，央行將通膨發展情勢列為首要抉
擇貨幣政策指標。本行認為近期台灣的通膨情勢

加快推出相關配套措施，宣佈設立5兆
韓元半導體專項基金，重點投資芯片材

業，並提供相關供應商總額5兆韓元貿

年計畫，促進大企業和較小供應商合作
如半導體研發、測試及生產等領域。再
者費外成為對經濟貢獻的推手之一。

並非主要來自供給測壓力，主要是考量核心CPI 已
連續四個月維持在 2%以上，搭配民間消費對 GDP

的貢獻逐季顯現 ，這表明持續的內需復甦正在產
生需求側通膨壓力 。另一方面根據台灣行政院院
長表示 ，普發現金經費已納入總預算 ，立法院預
算審查進度將定奪普發現金 1萬的時點，目前可能
的時間最快在2027農曆春節（2月4日至10日）前
後，抑或在元宵節（2月20日）前。而根據央行預
估1Q27整體物價增速仍高於 2%，若疊加現金補
貼的加持 ，不排除物價增幅將更高於原預期水準 。
其次央行聲明第二個觀察重點 : 主要經濟體貨幣政
策緊縮程度。美歐日央行9月已升息一碼，美聯準
會年底尚有升息一碼的可能性 ，韓國央行 7、8月
連續性升息後，11月與1Q27都有可能再度升息。
多國相繼貨幣政策緊縮態勢 ，或增加台灣央行貨
幣政策抉擇的壓力 。而若台灣基準利率與他國利
差擴大 ，也可能引發資金外流 ，也不利央行關心
的金融情勢發展 。因此綜合來看 ，本行預期 12月
央行理監事會議有可能決議升息半碼至2.125%。
《投資關鍵-聚焦穩健基本面有望抵禦高利環境》
展望後市，美財政部有意擴大美債回購規模緩和債

務壓力，美聯準會9月升息一碼至3.75%-4.0%，年
底不排除還有一碼的升息空間，但從通膨與景氣預
估路徑來看，美聯準會的升息舉措並非典型的升息
循環，而是「不承諾路徑的風險管理式升息」。
回到對新興亞洲貨幣政策路徑來看，或許促使部分
新興亞洲央行政策光譜往緊縮端推移，但不改變原
具降息需求新亞國家的寬鬆方向，整體新亞國家貨
幣政策走向分歧。更重要的是決定新興亞洲貨幣政
策的考量點，如台灣與韓國主要是因應景氣回溫而
貨幣政策緊縮，但像無財政補貼物價致緊縮貨幣政
策國家如菲律賓，再者還有經濟成動能尚不穩固或
通膨可控，貨幣政策按兵不動的如中國、泰國等。

經驗可發現 ，期中選舉年度的新興亞洲股市 9月出
現回檔的幅度與機率，普遍高於非期中選舉年度。

• 高盛預期投行三大業務呈週期
中期成長。科技(尤其是AI相關)

市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
金融 正向 AI驅動股債與併購復甦，資本市場將迎來週期中期健康成長，存貸款業務成長延續
1
《人工智慧(AI)基礎設施公司融資需求和市值的成
長在近期投資銀行收入成長中發揮了核心作用》
高盛預期投資銀行三大業務領域均會實現成長 ，並
處於週期中期。在諮詢業務方面，預期未來12個月
併購交易量將成長 19%；股權資本市場(ECM)方面，
近期成長主要得益於強勁的科技業 (尤其是 AI相關
產業)活動，其中科技類 ECM貢獻了年初至今成長
的約75%，高盛預期成長將持續 ，估計2026年美
國IPO籌資額將達到 2,250億美元的年度新高紀錄；
而債權資本市場 (DCM)的增長主要會由 2大因素推
動，(1)AI相關融資規模龐大 ，其中相當一部分預

計將轉化為 DCM的業務量 ，(2)基於對企業併購交
易量持正向看法 ，這亦將為 DCM提供強大支撐 。
因併購活動中有很大一部份會依賴債務融資。
高盛預估今年迄今為止，併購、股權資本市場和債
權資本市場交易量及費用的合計增長中，約有
分析師／陳嘉琳
研究範疇 金融／非核心消費
金融產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/9/11
資料來源：GS，2026/9/8，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/9
預計2026年美國IPO總募資額將創新高紀錄

吸納量
重點摘要
2
50~55%是由AI相關交易驅動的 。眼下Claude開發
商Anthropic何時上市就成為市場焦點 ，目前市場
推估最快10月中展開首次公開發行(IPO)的推介活動，
並可能在11月美國期中選舉前數日掛牌上市 。此案
被 視 為 史 上 規 模 最 大 的 IPO 案之一 ， 緊 接 在
Anthropic之後還會有OpenAI。
《市場預期股票市場成長依然強勁 ，固定收益 、外
匯及大宗商品(FICC)市場成長放緩》
銀行交易收入能否延續今年上半年強勁表現 ，其持
續性取決於市場水平 、融資利差和波動性 。從結構
上來看，可交易流通股數的增長帶動了交易量 ，進
而推升了客戶資金融通該持股所需的槓桿額度 ，通

常透過大宗經紀業務進行。
正向的週期性效益包括：股票融資利差是 2020年第
三季的三倍 ，反映出股票槓桿需求已超越產業產能；
首度公開發行(IPO)的回溫活絡了次級市場交易；個
股波動率升幅高於大盤指數波動率 ，為客戶創造更
大的超額報酬(Alpha)機會並提升參與度。故高盛預
期2026~2028年美國大型銀行股票市場複合年增長
率將達到 5~7%，但同期間 FICC市場的複合年增長
約為2~4%，這是因為市場波動性較低 ，限制了中
介活動。
《規模效應 、資產負債表彈性驅動資本市場收入步
入健康增長》
自 2019 年 以 來 資 本 市 場 收 入 加 速 成 長， 2019 至
2026年產業費用池複合年增率 (CAGR)將達 11%，
高盛預測 2026至2028年將放緩至 4%，雖說放緩但
仍維持健康增長 。這項成長主要歸功於可交易資產
池的擴張 ，以及全球債務和股權市場規模的擴大 ，
而非利潤率的提高，大型銀行交易資產自 2019年以
來成長約 110%，同期利潤率基本持平 。此外，自

2025年11月補充槓桿率 (SLR)改革實施以來 ，銀行
受惠於資產負債表更大的彈性 ，槓桿率 (平均資產 /
權益)在2019年到2026年上半年明顯上升 。同時，
經營槓桿上升帶來規模經濟效益 ，使資本市場業務
的效率比率 (營運成本/收入)下降，這是因為更高的
固定成本轉化為規模經濟效益 。最終，使資本市場
業務的股東權益報酬率(ROE)上升。

結論
◼ 先進封裝產能供不應求 ，已成AI產業發展瓶頸： 五大晶圓代工廠 2026年資本支出年增約 61%，上行週期
可延伸至2027-2028年，長達2-3年以上，不同於過往僅約1年的循環。台積電 CoWoS 月產能上修尤其顯著
，2026年由去年35kwpm倍增至70kwpm；OSAT則於2026-2027年以翻倍速度擴產，主因AI晶片需求強勁。
台灣設備商2027年可望受惠台積電 、日月光擴產；展望 2030年，隨在地化採購與長期資本支出展望正向
，台廠仍有逾1倍成長空間。
◼ 先進封裝為技術與成本效益的必然趨勢,NVIDIA GPU Roadmap揭示三大發展方向：先進封裝從技術角度
、成本效益層面皆已成為必須發展的方向，從NVIDIA最新GPU Roadmap觀察，未來先進封裝提升晶片效
能將呈現三大趨勢：(1)晶片尺寸持續放大；(2)TDP每個世代接近倍增；(3)採用堆疊技術縮短die間互連距
離，提升單位電晶體密度。
◼ 台廠受惠層次分明 ，2027年解翹曲、解熱設備為主軸 ，2028年新製程導入設備商產值大幅提升： 2027年

隨產能擴張，CoWoS原設備商可望受惠，惟解翹曲、解熱設備商TAM成長更佳，因其原非CoWoS主要設
備，隨晶片尺寸與TDP攀升已轉為必需品 。2028年隨CoPoS、SoIC逐步導入，台廠設備商kwpm產值可望
大幅提升，個別公司則透過站點數增加、附加價值提升，帶動ASP成長10%至100%不等。

記憶體原廠因整體產業需求強勁 ，均以接近翻倍的水準積極擴產 ；而主要邏輯晶圓代工廠台積電 ，雖年增幅
僅約50%，但主因在去年創新高的基期下仍能維持激進擴產步調，顯示產業仍處於上升週期。
基期墊高增速趨緩、但五大廠仍積極擴產，上行週期尾部效應更長：展望2027-2028年，雖然年增幅因基期墊
高而逐步趨緩，但全球五大晶圓代工廠仍持續積極擴充產能，顯示本輪資本支出上行週期，相較過往僅約1年
的循環，將展現更長的尾部效應，持續期間可望延伸至2-3年甚至更長的週期性。

資本支出強勁，且具長尾效應，半導體設備商將持續受惠
資料來源：SEMI；福邦投顧整理
整體半導體設備市場規模與成長幅度同步放大：根據SEMI預估2026年全球半導體製造設備銷售額達1,659億美
元，較2025年成長23.2%，創歷史新高；在AI需求驅動下，成長動能可望延續至 2028年，達2,295億美元，創
連續五年成長新紀錄。隨元件複雜度提升、先進封裝加速導入，以及AI與HBM對效能與可靠度要求提高，成
長動能可望延續至2028年，測試設備規模上看208億美元，組裝與封裝設備上看86億美元。
前段五大龍頭設備廠為主要受惠者，2026-28年營收成長CAGR上看50-60%：前段晶圓廠設備占整體設備支出
比重約8-9成，其中以ASML、Lam、AMAT、TEL、KLA等前段設備龍頭為主。這些設備廠主要負責曝光、顯
影、蝕刻、沉積、清洗等關鍵前段製程，憑藉對材料與製程的深厚know-how，與晶圓廠維持20年以上緊密合
作，可望在資本支出加速擴張下享有規模優勢帶動的營收成長。

7【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
摩爾定律失效加劇 ，先進封裝將成為突破算力瓶頸的關鍵
資料來源：AMD；AI and Memory Wall；福邦投顧整理
成本增幅遠超效能增益 ，先進封裝成為更具效益的替代路徑： 摩爾定律的微縮效益近年明顯趨緩 ，3nm晶圓
成本約2萬美元，2nm晶圓成本上看3萬美元，成本增幅約達50%，但電晶體密度僅提升不到1.15倍，顯示摩爾
定律失效情況較過往更加嚴重 。相較之下，先進封裝既可異質整合又可降低成本 ，成為比製程微縮更具效益
的成長路徑。
記憶體牆問題須靠HBM突破，先進封裝為唯一解方：除先進製程投資報酬率下滑外，AI算力成長速度已明顯
快於DRAM頻寬與互連傳輸能力，系統瓶頸逐步由「算不夠快」轉向「資料送不夠快」，形成 Memory Wall
，而這道瓶頸並非邏輯製程微縮能解決 。唯透過先進封裝將HBM以更短的垂直互連距離、更高密度整合至運
算核心周邊，才能有效提升記憶體頻寬 、突破記憶體牆限制 ，這是先進封裝在晶片效能提升上 ，角色不同於

過去的核心原因。
圖4、摩爾定律失效，電晶體數量無法隨著製程微縮翻倍 圖5、記憶體牆成為晶片算力最大瓶頸

半導體後段先進封裝廠近年將以翻倍速度擴增產能

後段先進封裝需求緊俏程度更甚先進製程：不僅前段製程在擴產上積極，台積電的後段先進封裝更以倍增的成長
幅度，今年產能仍持續大幅上修 ，隨AI晶片全面朝先進封裝發展 ，除GPU、ASIC外，CPU亦開始導入先進封裝
，需求端則受惠Agentic AI帶動任務調度需求大幅增加，CPU與GPU搭配比例由8:1提升至1:1，進一步推升CPU先
進封裝需求；同時，ASIC晶片亦隨終端推論需求持續強勁，帶動CSP加速建置與放量。

一步上修至205kwpm，OSAT則已規劃擴產至70kwpm，OSAT則持續受惠台積電外包oS產能，隨類CoWoS產能良

資本支出上行+在地化滲透率倍增，台灣封裝設備廠業績成長可期
資料來源：台積電、福邦投顧整理預估
台廠設備商將受惠在地化政策 ，滲透率具倍數成長空間： 根據台積電於2030年設定的在地化採購目標 ，原物
料、零配件、後段設備的在地採購比率分別為50%、68%、38%。以後段設備而言，現階段本土設備商占台積
電後段封裝資本支出比重約僅16-18%，對照2030年38%的目標，長期滲透率仍有逾1倍。台廠除可受惠台積電
擴產本身外，更有望受惠於在地化政策持續深化。
台積電資本支出長期上行趨勢確立 ，台廠設備商TAM有望成長逾一倍以上：AI需求持續強勁且產能上無法滿
足客戶需求，將加速建廠、同時未來三年合計CAPEX將顯著高於過去三年。若進一步假設台積電2027-2030年
間累計資本支出達3,000-4,000億美元規模，反推隱含CAGR約14.3%。在滲透率提升與資本支出總量成長雙重
動能疊加下，將仍有倍增以上的成長空間。

### Page 10

先進封裝技術趨勢一、晶片尺寸放大

### Page 11

11【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
NV Roadmap顯示晶片尺寸及效能持續提升，CoWoS需求因應而生
資料來源：台積電、SemiAnalysis；福邦投顧整理
NV引領晶片尺寸放大趨勢 ，TDP也呈現世代倍增： 作為業界最早採用新硬體技術的晶片設計廠商 ，NV的技
術路徑往往領先ASIC廠商1-2個世代。透過NV Roadmap觀察，未來晶片將持續整合更多 Compute、I/O die與
HBM，使單顆封裝體的整體尺寸持續放大 ，以換取更高算力與效能表現 。伴隨晶片尺寸放大，TDP呈現顯著
跳升，每一世代接近倍增，解熱方案勢必由伺服器端逐步內移至晶片層級。
空間與互聯限制下，3D垂直堆疊搭配混合鍵合成最適解方： 隨著單顆晶片須搭載更多HBM，同時整合SRAM
與Compute die以追求算力最佳化，平面整合方式已面臨空間瓶頸，垂直堆疊遂成為突破限制的最適路徑，並
可藉由混合鍵合技術，將die與die間的互聯效率提升至最佳水準。

### Page 12

12【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
台積電 CoWoS 中介層技術比較
CoWoS-S CoWoS-R CoWoS-L
中介層 矽 有機材料製作重佈線層 有機材料製作重佈線層+崁入矽橋(LSI)連接die
成本 高 低 中
成熟度 高 中 低
信號完整度 中 低 高
光罩尺寸 3.3x 3.3x >3.3x
優勢 技術最成熟，線路可以做到最細，訊號傳輸最穩定。 RDL線路密度較低，訊號傳輸延遲略高於S，但有機材料中介層有更好的機械強度。 Die to Die之間通過矽橋連結，其餘地方則用有機材料做成中介層，綜合S傳輸性能好+R成本低、機械性佳的特徵。
劣勢 受限於光罩尺寸，最大僅能到858mm²，同時材質脆且貴。 傳輸效率差 製造過程容易因材料CTE差異而在RDL層數增加、面積放大下發生翹曲問題。
示意圖
表2、CoWoS – S / R / L 技術差異分析
資料來源：富果、福邦投顧整理
矽 有機材料 混合
CoWoS-L兼具成本與量產優勢，成台積電擴產主流：為因應3.3x以上reticle size的量產需求，台積電

後續擴產均以CoWoS-L製程為主。相較全矽中介層 ，CoWoS-L成本更低，同時可做到比CoWoS-R更
細L/S，搭配有機材料更高的機械強度，更適合現行dual die、且搭載更多I/O pitch的HBM4等元件的
大面積晶片應用。

### Page 13

13【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
台積電持續推出更大封裝平台 ，將採面板級封裝因應尺寸放大趨勢
資料來源：台積電；福邦投顧整理
面積使用效率下滑 ，推動CoPoS成為下一步解方： 隨著晶片尺寸持續放大 ，傳統圓形晶圓的面積使用效率開始
下滑，為推展CoPoS的關鍵原因。以NVIDIA最新晶片5.5x reticle size計算，每片300mm晶圓約可切出7塊中介層
，若轉換為方形面板則可提升至約 16塊，使用面積比重可由 45%大幅提升至81%，進而有效降低單位成本 ，為
客戶同時達成降本與效能提升的目標。
圖10、台積電2030年封裝尺寸將再成長1x以上 圖11、Panel 可以增加面積效率、降低成本

### Page 14

14【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
台積電CoPoS 圓轉方的兩大技術難點：均勻度 、Carrier 結構轉換
資料來源：辛耘、福邦投顧整理
圓轉方使塗佈均勻度控制困難： 圓形基板由中心到邊緣的距離等長 ，在Coating製程時可運用離心力，使材料
均勻的塗布在Wafer上；方形基板則因四個角落與邊緣的距離不一致，均勻度控制難度大幅提升。Coating若無
法均勻，亦將連帶加劇後續清洗、烘烤製程中受熱均勻度的控制難度，形成連鎖性的良率挑戰。
TBDB結構轉為方形，考驗晶圓減薄與重佈線的成敗：TBDB設備扮演承上啟下的物理支撐角色，先進封裝製
程高度仰賴晶圓減薄與重佈線兩道工序 ，若無TBDB設備提供完美支撐 ，超薄基板在製程中將面臨結構性
crack的災難性風險。在此環節，台廠辛耘已切入release layer塗佈與清洗設備，卡位方形基板轉換過程中的關
鍵站點。
圖12、Panel 易造成邊角堆積 圖13、TBDB、release layer 示意圖

### Page 15

15【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
台積電CoPoS相對Intel的EMIB-T封裝平台仍是最佳解
資料來源：台積電；Intel；TrendForce；福邦投顧整理
CoPoS量產時程對應Feynman，封裝形式以Carrier為主流方向： 根據台積電說法 ，CoPoS量產時程預計落在
2H28，與最大尺寸晶片Feynman的量產時程相對應，因此推論Feynman可能為首度採用CoPoS的晶片。封裝形
式目前仍有諸多討論，但最有機會量產的形式是作為RDL載體的Carrier改成310*310mm的Panel。
EMIB-T良率仍待成熟 ，CoPoS仍為效能表現最佳的封裝平台： EMIB雖號稱良率達90%，惟該數字並未涵蓋
載板，目前已知載板良率仍低於50%，尚未達到可量產水準。相較之下，CoPoS仍是封裝效能表現更佳的平台
，其interposer可實現更密的佈線密度 、容納更多電容設計 ，因此在需要高功率 、高I/O的高階晶片應用中 。
EMIB則因少掉中介層降低封裝成本，部分CSP已經轉向開始將自家次要產品逐漸轉移至該平台。

### Page 20

20【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
晶片尺寸持續放大，台廠烤箱抗翹曲方案價值浮現
資料來源：志聖；印能；福邦投顧整理
翹曲問題成因複雜 ，五大因素呈非線性惡化： 翹曲問題主要來自不同封裝材料的 CTE存在差異，導致加熱過
程中材料之間產生應力差 ，進而造成材料彎曲 。此問題會隨著 (1)Panel面積增加、(2)材料種類增加 、(3)材料
CTE差距擴大、(4)厚度減薄、(5)RDL層數增加等因素呈非線性提升 。翹曲問題若未有效解決，將導致銅線、
開孔位置偏差，或使Pad間黏接處斷裂，增加晶片失效風險，進而影響良率與性能表現。
加熱過程無所不在，志聖、印能各有布局：先進封裝製程中，舉凡Molding、固化、退火、回焊、Underfill等
站點皆須經歷加熱過程 ，翹曲問題便是在這些加熱過程中逐步產生 。台灣設備商中，志聖、印能均有抗翹曲
烤箱解決方案，惟對應站點有所不同。志聖的烤箱主要應用於Molding、固化、退火等加熱站點；印能則主要
聚焦於Underfill段的加熱、退火過程。

志聖 印能
產品 解翹曲烤箱 WSAS
製程站點 Molding、固化、退火 Underfill de-void
處理方式 直接熱加壓

烘烤中，氣囊會頂著晶片。

### Page 21

21【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
晶片持續增加功耗讓散熱解方往封裝內移 ，台廠掌握核心供應地位
資料來源：台積電；TrendForce；健策；一詮；福邦投顧整理
熱阻抗成效能瓶頸 ，散熱方案由伺服器端逐步內移至晶片層級： 熱阻抗是影響晶片使用過程中效能能否發揮
至最大化的關鍵要素 ，因此晶片設計商逐步將散熱方案從伺服器端 、板端，一路推進至晶片內部，以應對單
顆晶片已超過1500W的極高功耗，使晶片效能得以持續提升。目前主要ASIC、GPU在今、明兩年推出的新世
代產品中，功耗幾乎皆已突破1500W以上，普遍採用TIM+Lid+Stiffener等1-2片式的散熱解法。
竑騰貼合表面處理優於萬潤 ，可望在後續世代取得更高市占： 台廠中負責TIM+Lid+Stiffener等貼合設備的供
應商，主要為萬潤與竑騰 ，其中萬潤主要負責貼合製程 。除石墨烯材質的 TIM貼合以萬潤佔比較高外 ，其餘
如Lid、Stiffener、銦片材質的貼合 ，則以竑騰佔比更高 ，主因其貼合表面處理能力優於競爭對手 ，可使貼合

表面處理得更為平整，讓銦片能更完整貼附於晶片上，進而使解熱效率優於萬潤。

CoPoS量價齊揚，台廠後段封裝設備商全鏈受惠
資料來源：各家公司資料；福邦投顧預估
ASP反映成本與功能增量 ，漲幅因公司而異： CoPoS轉換帶動ASP反映10-20%成本上漲，疊加功能增加後漲幅
可達50-100%。均華、萬潤偏向反映成本；弘塑 、辛耘隨基板轉方形 、清洗難度提升，帶動設備升級；志聖藉
複合功能烤箱提升附加價值；倍利科則憑翹曲處理能力創造價值。
站點數量同步增加：志聖在CoP、OSA ToS段站點數量增加達1倍，並整合更多Combo機；萬潤持續送樣台積電
CoP段，滲透率有望提升；倍利科則憑X-Ray透視檢測優勢，應用範圍可望從Carrier延伸至更多複雜疊構站點。

### Page 23

先進封裝趨勢二、晶片採 SoIC 堆疊提升效能

### Page 24

24【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
SoIC為單位密度提升電晶體數量的最佳解
資料來源：AMD；TrendForce；福邦投顧整理預估
SoIC透過大幅提升晶粒間互連密度 ，突破傳統封裝的傳輸瓶頸： 根據AMD資料顯示，運用SoIC D2D連接可
提供15倍互連密度、3倍能源效率，透過縮短晶粒間傳輸距離、增加訊號通道，大幅降低資料傳輸延遲與功耗
。隨AI晶片對die、Cache、Memory間高速資料交換需求提升，SoIC將成為提升晶片整體效能的重要技術。
先進製程微縮效益降低，SoIC透過3D堆疊可更具成本效益地提升電晶體密度：因單純依賴製程微縮提升電晶
體密度的成本效益縮小；相較之下 SoIC可透過垂直堆疊晶粒 ，增加單位封裝面積可整合的電晶體數量 ，並隨
堆疊層數增加進一步提升電晶體密度，成為延續晶片算力成長更具成本效益的路徑。
圖17、SoIC 主要應用於 Die to Die 互聯

### Page 25

25【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
SoIC疊構將使晶片效能大幅提升，可望成2.5D平台主流搭配方案
資料來源：Aletheia；TrendForce；AMD；Hot Chips；福邦投顧整理預估
SoIC大幅提升晶片效能，客戶群持續擴大採用：SoIC技術可將同質、異質晶片進行整合並堆疊，透過疊構縮
短die之間的距離 ，增加傳輸速度 ，同時提升單位電晶體密度 。目前主要採用客戶包括 Apple M5/M6、AMD
MI350/400/450，均透過SoIC製程以D2W方式進行整合。
CPO良率仍待突破 ，NVIDIA導入可望帶動SoIC產能大幅成長： 2027年SoIC產能仍受CPO良率影響，預估月
產能約落在40kwpm。2028年隨NV新產品推出將大量於NVLink 8、Spectrum逐步導入CPO架構，加上Feynman
的D2D整合需求 ，以及未來 ASIC、LPU有望採用 SRAM堆疊 ，SoIC應用範圍可望持續擴大；隨主要客戶
NVIDIA開始採用，產能有望迎來大幅成長。

打入類前段組裝工藝製程，台廠設備商站點含金量升級
資料來源： 各家公司資料 ；福邦投顧整理預估
對準壓合、表面平整為兩大關鍵：SoIC製程依序為KGD分選→表面磨平→表面洗乾淨→表面活化→對準→鍵
合→退火→後續整合。其中最關鍵的難點在於對準與壓平 ，對準壓合主要供應商為歐洲設備大廠 BESI；而
台廠則主要切入另一重要製程清洗。
台廠跨入類前段製程站點，技術能力大幅提升：SoIC製程在整體封裝技術中最接近前段晶圓廠的精細度與潔
淨度要求，這也是打入此製程更重要的意涵所在，隨SoIC需求擴大，有機會逐步跨入晶圓前段更多製程站點
，提升自身在供應鏈中的技術含金量與滲透深度。

   公司簡介 公司成立於 1981 年，主要經營氟素樹脂材料相關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品生產及銷售，產品主要應用於半導體、面板高純化學品系統設備產品線。
